### Page 16

TO P I C  9 | N ATU R E &  C AR B O N  MAR K ETS
Nature and carbon markets have key roles to play in supporting climate action
Carbon credit discussions will centre on government policies to boost demand and corporate engagement, with much of the formal UN architecture now in place. If carbon markets can scale,
the benefits would be wide-ranging (e.g. supporting emissions targets and providing finance for developing countries). Credibility and greenwashing concerns remain key risks however; we

expect prices to diverge in favour of high-integrity credits. The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF) now has ~$7.8bn of sponsor capital against a $10bn end-2026 target.
Sources: Tropical Forest Forever Facility, Coalition to Grow Carbon Markets
16
Voluntary carbon market development
Türkiye has stated a focus on carbon markets at COP31, particularly on government actions to
boost demand, highlighting the potential for carbon markets to unlock more than $50bn in

additional annual finance for developing nations. The Coalition to Grow Carbon Markets
(made up of 13 governments) has announced it will unveil a “Policy Playbook” at COP31 to
support market development. While much of Article 6 (carbon credits under UN process) has
moved to delivery, countries will aim to reach agreement on the funding model for Article 6.2
at COP31. Further carbon market progress stands to have multiple benefits; it can support
developed countries in hitting climate goals while reducing immediate decarbonisation

requirements, while also providing finance and supporting ecosystems in developing ones.
Understanding the pushback
Carbon markets have seen pushback over credit integrity (including regulatory scrutiny) and
the risk that offsets delay domestic cuts. UN involvement is needed to enforce common
accounting and integrity rules, such as the Article 6 rulebook finalised at COP29 and due for
review in 2028, that stop the same reduction being claimed twice. Without that backstop,

fragmented bilateral and voluntary schemes risk eroding the trust needed to scale the market.
Update on the TFFF
The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF), launched at COP30 last year, aims for $10bn of
sponsor contributions by year-end. It pays tropical-forest countries per hectare conserved,
discounts payments for each hectare lost or degraded, and directs at least 20% to Indigenous
Peoples and local communities. Its Charter was adopted on 22 July 2026 and received a

tailwind following the re-election of Brazilian President Lula, who is a key supporter. Shortly
after Lula’s re-election, the Netherlands pledged up to $500mn to the fund, bringing the total
pledged to $7.8bn. Further financing commitments are key, with Norway’s $3bn contribution
conditional on the fund reaching at least $10bn by the end of 2026.

TO P I C  1 0 | P AC I F I C , O C EANS  & S EA L EV EL  R I S E
Oceans will be a priority item, with Australia promising to champion Pacific interests
Australia’s ask for ceding the hosting was a Pacific pre-COP, and it has promised to continue championing Pacific interests at COP31. The Taku Pakasoa Declaration, signed at the pre-COP,

reflects Pacific priorities, calling for faster emissions cuts, more adaptation finance and financial system reform. The climate-ocean nexus will also be a focus, including investable solutions.
Championing Pacific interests is a key focus for Australia
As part of the hosting compromise, Australia co-hosted the official pre-COP with Fiji and
Tuvalu (5–8 Oct), attended by countries including Germany, the UAE and India (though some
questioned the low number of international leaders who attended). Australia has pledged to

keep championing Pacific interests as President of the Negotiations.
Taku Pakasoa Declaration reflects Pacific Priorities
Signed on 6 Oct at the pre-COP, it calls for faster emissions cuts, a substantial increase in
climate finance (particularly for adaptation in capacity-constrained countries), reform of the
international financial architecture, and for elevating the ocean-climate nexus. It also
welcomes the 35% electrification goal.
The challenges of rising sea levels are gaining increasing prominence

Sea levels in the region are rising faster than the global average, and half of Pacific Island
infrastructure lies within 500 metres of the coast. The UN General Assembly adopted the UN
Declaration on Sea Level Rise by consensus on 24 Sept; global sea levels rose nearly 6mm in
2024, the highest annual increase on record.
Oceans are key to climate stability, but more finance is needed
Pacific nations emphasise the critical role of oceans as a natural environmental regulator.

They absorb 30% of all carbon dioxide emissions (largest carbon sink) and capture 90% of the
excess heat generated by these emissions. Investor interest in sustainable ocean solutions is
growing, with 2026 YTD blue bond issuance already an annual record, yet bond issuance
supporting UN SDG 14: Life Below Water trails many other categories; scaling this up will be a
focus at COP31. A commitment to delivering “tangible, bankable ocean outcomes” at COP31 is

one of 13 points included as part of the Trabzon Ocean Call to Action unveiled by Türkiye,
while the ORRAA has unveiled a Blue Climate Finance Pathway.
Sources: Taku Pakasoa Declaration (UNFCCC), UN Declaration on Sea Level Rise

F U TU R E O F  C O P
Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027, though questions over how COP will evolve are in focus
COP has shifted from setting targets to implementation. Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027, where we expect a focus on delivering commitments and climate finance access, reflecting African
priorities. COP33 falls to Asia-Pacific in 2028, with China reportedly considering a bid and South Korea potentially interested. COP31–33 will test delivery in a fragmented world, with a second

Global Stocktake concluding in 2028. Its outcome should inform countries’ next national climate plans (NDCs, their emissions-reduction pledges), due in 2030.

AP P END I X  |   COP  TER MS
Appendix A: Key COP terms used in this report
This appendix explains the main COP process terms and negotiating concepts used throughout the report. Definitions are summarised for non-specialist readers and reflect how each term is
used in this deck.
Sources: UNFCCC; COP30 and COP31 Presidency documents; and the other sources referenced in this report
19
Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
TERM DEFINITION

Action Agenda Voluntary, non-binding pledges and initiatives launched alongside the formal talks. It is separate from negotiated decisions.
Annex II / Non-Annex I UNFCCC country classification. Annex II covers developed economies; Non-Annex I covers most other countries, including China and India.
Article 6 Paris Agreement framework for international carbon markets. Article 6.2 covers bilateral trading of carbon credits between countries.
Article 9

Paris Agreement finance provisions. Article 9.1 covers developed
countries’ obligation to provide finance. COP30 set a two-year work
programme for 2026-27.
Baku to Belém Roadmap
to $1.3T
Roadmap to scale climate finance to $1.3tn a year by 2035 from all
sources, inclusive of the $300bn NCQG.
Bonn sessions Mid-year technical talks of the UNFCCC Subsidiary Bodies (e.g. SB64) in Bonn, Germany, which prepare the ground for COP discussions.
Brazil fossil fuel roadmap COP30 Presidency’s voluntary roadmap for transitioning away from fossil fuels, pursued outside the formal UN negotiations.

Consensus How COP decisions are taken: adopted when no party formally objects, so a single party can theoretically block or dilute an outcome.
COP / COPs Conference of the Parties: the UNFCCC’s annual decision-making summit, where nearly 200 parties negotiate climate action.
COP Presidency The host country’s team that chairs the conference. For COP31, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum is COP President and leads the Action Agenda.
TERM DEFINITION
Final COP text Decisions adopted by consensus at the close of the COP, setting its formal outcome.

Formal agenda Negotiated items on the official COP agenda, decided by consensus. Separate from the voluntary Action Agenda.
Global Goal on
Adaptation (GGA)
Paris Agreement goal to strengthen resilience. COP30 adopted 59
indicators; the link to finance is contested.
Global Stocktake (GST) Five-yearly review of progress under the Paris Agreement. The second runs from COP31 to COP33 (2028).
Just transition
mechanism
Belém Action Mechanism agreed at COP30, focused on fairness in the

shift to a low-carbon economy.
Loss and damage fund Fund set up at COP27 and operationalised in 2023 to support vulnerable developing countries.
NCQG New Collective Quantified Goal (COP29): at least $300bn a year for developing countries by 2035, within a $1.3tn ambition.
NDC Nationally determined contribution: each country’s national climate plan, with emissions targets and actions, updated every five years.
Negotiating groups Blocs that coordinate national positions, such as the G77 & China, the EU, LDCs and AOSIS.

Paris Agreement 2015 treaty to hold warming well below 2°C and pursue 1.5°C, with national climate plans (NDCs) as its core mechanism.
Pre-COP Ministerial meeting ahead of the COP to test positions and build consensus. For COP31, Australia co-hosted a Pacific pre-COP.
President of Negotiations COP31 role held by Australia’s Chris Bowen, who drafts final texts and steers government-to-government talks.
UNFCCC UN Framework Convention on Climate Change: the 1992 treaty behind the climate process. Its parties meet annually at the COP.

Appendix C: The level of alarm over global warming has begun to move higher (again)
As part of our monthly Climate & Sustainability Survey, we ask consumers in the UK, US, Germany, France, Spain and Italy for their views on global warming. In Q3 2026, we recorded a notable
rebound in alarm regarding the pace of global warming, particularly in the UK and Europe following a summer of climate extremes. The level of alarm had begun to dip globally post the 2024 US

presidential election in November.

Document 385: New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-12)

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• As polls suggested, the election was a nailbiter
that has not yet been resolved. Election night
results show a lead to the centre-right on
paper in a significantly enlarged parliament.
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First
and ACT won 67 seats. The centre-left bloc
of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti Māori won 57
seats. There was a swing away from National
to its coalition partners while the centre left
grouping picked up a few seats.

• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling
and will enter parliament with at least 7 seats,
reducing what could have been a larger swing
to the centre-left grouping.
• The size of parliament will expand by around 9
seats as National held onto a large share of its
electorate votes, entitling it to more seats than
implied by its party vote share. As a result,
a bloc would require 66 seats to command a
majority in parliament.
• Several senior National ministers have not

been re-elected, including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. She will continue in a caretaker
role but will need to be replaced in any new
centre-right government.
• Significant uncertainty about the result exists.
National might lose perhaps two marginal
electorates in the final analysis. There are
400,000 special votes to count which may
shift the list seat allocations as these tend
to reduce the share of the centre-right bloc
by 1.6-1.7ppts. Hence, when all is said and

done, the centre-right’s 10 seat advantage on
election night could reduce by 1-4 seats.
• Should this play out the centre-right would not
have sufficient seats to reach a majority in an
enlarged house and would require the support
of the Opportunity Party to govern.
• The final election outcome will not be
known until 27 November. Serious coalition
negotiations likely won’t begin until then
and delays could occur. Should recounts be
required, some further delay is possible.

• Based on these results, we see a higher
probability of a centre-right government
ultimately emerging that likely will require
the support of the Opportunity Party in some
form. The Opportunity Party likely won’t enter
Cabinet but may offer support on confidence
and supply in exchange for policy concessions.
• But a centre-left government can’t be ruled
out if special votes heavily favour the centre
left bloc, and especially if an agreement
between the Opportunity Party and the centre-

right parties can’t be reached.
• Regardless of the final count, the results
suggest a government with weak support and
ongoing vulnerabilities. Hence the prospect of
controversial large changes may be modest.
For a centre-right government that might
limit the extent of fiscal consolidation, while
the centre left’s plans for larger scale tax and
spending initiatives could be trimmed back.
• A centre-right government won’t change the
RBNZ mandate while a centre-left government

likely still will. A new finance minister will be
required with the possibility of ACT leader
David Seymour taking on that role.

New Zealand 2026 General Election:
Preliminary results and next steps.
9 Nov 2026 |  Westpac Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz
Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
The result.
The preliminary results of the 2026 General Election,
based on the election night count, are set out in the table
above. In summary:
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First and ACT
won 67 seats.
• The centre-left bloc of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti
Māori won 57 seats.
• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling and will
enter parliament with 7 seats.
These results indicate a swing away from National to its

coalition partners while the centre-left grouping picked
up a few seats. The big gainer was the Opportunity Party
who perhaps took a big share of what otherwise could
have been a larger swing to the centre-left grouping.
The size of parliament will expand by around nine seats,
as National held onto a large share of its electorate
votes and hence has more seats than it would have been
entitled to based on its party vote share. As a result, a
governing bloc would require 66 seats to command a

majority in parliament.
Several of the electorate seats are very tight (see Table
2), so election night majorities could easily be overturned
once special votes are counted. Of particular relevance
is the seat of Wairarapa, where the National candidate is
leading the Labour candidate by just 139 votes. If Labour
were to win that seat (specials favoured Labour in 2020
but National in 2023), all else equal the centre-right seat
count would decline to 66 seats in what would become

a 130-seat Parliament – just enough to command a
majority. But if either NZ First or ACT were to lose a single
party vote seat to the left bloc once specials are counted
then that majority would disappear.
On that score, historically the election night results have
tended to overstate the support of the centre-right bloc
and understate the support for parties on the left of
the political spectrum. For example, over the previous
three elections (2017, 2020, 2023), the current centre-

right bloc’s vote share has declined 1.9%, 1.7% and 1.6%
respectively following the counting of special votes, with
the National Party losing 2 seats on each occasion once
the official results were declared (Labour, the Greens and
Te Pāti Māori have benefited). On this occasion, given its
overhang, a lower party vote share would not lower the
National Party’s final vote count, although it could alter
the size of its overhang and thus the number of seats

required to command a majority. In recent elections both
NZ First and ACT have seen their vote shares decline
following the counting of special votes.
The process from here.
Official results.
The official results will be declared on 27 November,
adding the results of an estimated 400,000 special votes
(with around a quarter of these cast overseas) to the
2.41m votes counted on election night.
We expect discussions between potential coalition
partners will begin immediately to start sketching out the

parameters of a coalition agreement. But given the tight
margins of the current vote count, no firm positions will
be agreed until the results are declared.
The writ and recounts.
After 27 November, the writ is scheduled to be returned
on 3 December – formally certifying who has been
elected. However, that date is subject to any recounts

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
that might be requested. Parliament is required to meet
no later than 14 January (six weeks after the return of the
writ), providing an opportunity for a new government
to test that it has the confidence of the House. It is
worth noting that Parliament can be summoned and
begin functioning even if coalition negotiations are still
occurring. If no coalition or confidence-and-supply
arrangement has been reached by then, negotiations can

simply continue and the incumbent Government remains
in office under the caretaker convention (Ministers in
the outgoing government retain executive authority
temporarily, even if they are no longer MPs).
Given that the Pre-Election Economic and Fiscal
Update was released in late September there is no legal
requirement for the Treasury to publish a Half Year
Economic and Fiscal Update (HYEFU) this year. And given
that it will be some weeks before a government is formed,

it seems unlikely that there will be a government in place
that would request that a HYEFU be produced despite
there being no statutory requirement (certainly there
would be little time to incorporate policy changes that
fall out of new coalition agreements, thus diminishing
its value). Once a government is formed, it will have an
opportunity to lay out its immediate priorities in the
Budget Policy Statement (BPS), which by law needs to
be released before the end of March next year. The new

government could decide to release an interim fiscal
update with the BPS if that was thought necessary.
Potential coalition outcomes.
We see three main likely outcomes:
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First.
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First with the support of the Opportunity Party
on confidence and supply.
• Some coalition of Labour, the Greens, Opportunity and
Te Pāti Māori, potentially with Opportunity and Te Pāti

Māori sitting outside the cabinet.
Our preview of the election discussed the policies of
the main political parties, while the figure on page 5
highlights some points of commonality with the centre-
right and centre-left blocs.
National, ACT and NZ First share a broadly pro-
development and deregulatory agenda, including support
for greater resource development, fewer regulatory
barriers and compulsory or expanded KiwiSaver. Beyond
that common core, National and ACT align particularly

closely on lower taxes and deregulation, while National
and NZ First share an emphasis on infrastructure, regional
development and law and order. ACT and NZ First favour
tighter immigration settings and tighter welfare policies.
The differences are clearest at the edges: ACT advocates
deeper tax cuts, smaller government and more market-
led provision, while NZ First places greater emphasis
on economic sovereignty, regional development and
targeted support for working New Zealanders. National

occupies more of the middle ground, combining fiscal
discipline and a pro-business agenda with increased
infrastructure and housing supply.
The Opportunity Party presents itself as a middle
alternative that can work with the left or the right
groupings. They have five bottom line policies they say
they need to support any new government. These include
competition reform through enhanced Commerce
Commission powers, more restrictions on political
lobbyists and donors, a ten-year plan of investment in the

healthcare sector, an end to bottom trawling in inshore
areas and a phase out elsewhere and for the Government
to recommit to New Zealand’s Paris Agreement
obligations. Their success in meeting these bottom
lines with a new government and their flexibility around
these requirements will be key to their ability to form an
agreement with either side. There is better congruence
of the Opportunity Party’s policies with the left than the
right – so progress here will be critical to watch should

their support be required to form a new government. We
suspect they will be very motivated to avoid the need for
a new election via a hung parliament should push come
to shove.

### Page 4

4
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Labour, the Greens and Opportunity share support
for increased public and social investment, a broader
tax base, greater infrastructure investment, stronger
competition policy, cheaper public transport and a
more active role for government. Labour and the Greens
overlap particularly on higher taxation, public investment
and stronger climate and environmental policy, while
Labour and Opportunity share an emphasis on social

investment and higher public-service wages. The Greens
and Opportunity both favour broader taxation and
stronger climate action, although their preferred tax
instruments differ. Their distinctive priorities are also
clear: Labour places more weight on worker protections
and restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate; the Greens on
environmental protection, progressive taxation and Te
Tiriti; and Opportunity on land-value taxation, housing
infrastructure, productivity, innovation and evidence-

based policy.

and differences.

Document 411: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-13).pdf

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 192026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.

CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer valueproposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

### Page 6

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FXand Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.
Should we explain how settlement work as it is very
interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?
Probably more relevant than having the stablecoin
market cap here I would say.
A more integrated post-trade process

### Page 7

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Corporates, card networks, and banks are integrating
stablecoins
Institutions are racing to capture stablecoin market share
– Card networks: Mastercard expanded its settlement capabilities to include
stablecoins, using USDC and Paxos-issued stablecoins.
– Institutions: Exploring stablecoins via developing in-house permissioned

blockchain experiments and/or exploring issuance of proprietary stablecoins.
– >140 companies and financial institutions helped launch Open USD
(OUSD) in Sep 2026. OUSD reached $799mn of supply in 2 weeks.
Signals shift toward consortium-backed/network-led digital dollar
models.
Are stablecoins posing a threat to traditional payment systems?
– Bullish: Stablecoins to surpass traditional rails by the 2030s (Chainalysis).
– ~17% of stablecoin-linked card volume is occurring across Visa’s

business and commercial card programs. Shows growing adoption of
stablecoins for settlement, treasury management and cross-border
commerce.
– Key concerns for banks: Fee pressure from stablecoin adoption →
bank outflows → higher reliance on wholesale funding → pressure on
net interest margins.
– Base case: However, Deutsche Bank equity analysts see more limited near-
term risk. Visa and Mastercard to remain dominant especially in advanced
markets.

– KYC, AML, sanctions screening, wallet monitoring, fraud controls and
jurisdiction-specific limits remain essential for banks.
Stablecoin transactions could match Visa and Mastercard transactions
sometime between 2031 and 2039 (Chainalysis).
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Chainalysis.
Shows projected years until crypto merchant transactions reach Visa scale.

### Page 9

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
GEOPOLITICS: Why stablecoins are a "Genius" idea for the US
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, US Treasury, Tether July 2026 report. Shows Major foreign holders of Treasury Securities.
Treasury data from June 2026.
Can sustain USD dominance
– 99% of stablecoins (by market cap) are USD-pegged.
– Stablecoins function primarily as a digital version of dollars

moving over blockchain rails, extending the dollar’s reach into
new digital environments.
– Reinforces dollar savings: The more firms invoice and transact in USD,
the more they save in USD assets. Stablecoins help preserve this
feedback loop by keeping businesses and individuals in dollar
payment rails.
Can support US debt, given share held by foreigners has fallen from
>50% in early 2000s to ~30% in early 2026.
– Stablecoin issuers total holdings: ~$170bn according to Allium x

Citizen.
– Tether: ~$141bn in direct and indirect exposure to US T-bills.
– Impact: BIS shows how $3.5bn inflow into dollar-backed stablecoins
lowers 3m T-bill yields by 2.5-3.5bp under normal conditions. Effect
more than doubles in periods of T-bill shortages.
– Forecasts: T-bills holding by stablecoin issuers could reach ~$1trn by
2028 (Treasury forecasts).
– Recent Fed SF research places it closer to $400bn by the end
of 2030, lower than the US government’s financing needs.

Stablecoin issuers’  demand for US debt could counterweight declining demand from China
Tether competes with Saudi Arabia and South Korea as major holders of US Treasuries
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, US Treasury Data. Shows Treasury
holdings, stablecoin issuers vs China.

### Page 10

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
US GENIUS ACT: Debate on rules around payment stablecoins one year on
GENIUS provisions
– Strict issuer requirements: Issuers can be private, non-bank entity, but must
meet strict federal or state requirements.
– Reserves: Requires 1:1 backing with US cash, 93 days T-bills, repos/reverse
repos, or money market funds holding those assets.
– AML/KYC/BSA compliance obligations to stablecoin issuers, aligning them

exams, and strong internal controls.
Next steps
– GENIUS takes effect no later than 18 Jan 2027, or 120 days after final
implementing regulations are issued.
– Ongoing pushback from US banks as to whether GENIUS ban on yield-
bearing stablecoins should extend to affiliate or third-party arrangements,
given many users do not hold stablecoins directly with the issuer.
– CLARITY ACT failed in Sep because of disagreements on how
exchanges/broader crypto market structure should handle stablecoins.

– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
There are two ways stablecoin yield can be paid; ongoing debate over the three-
party model
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Congressional Research Service.

RISKS: Still hotly discussed amongst policymakers and financial institutions
Bank liquidity risk
Loss of stable funding source for banks from deposit outflow (not offset by
partial flowback from stablecoin issuers’ reserve deposit)
System stability risk
Stablecoins can fail to maintain their $1 value during times of stress. Evidence shows
that USDC and Tether actually trade within 1% of their dollar value but prices can

diverge sharply during stress episodes.
Run on stablecoin issuer(s) in market stress / trust scenario triggering fire sales of
reserve assets and/or deposit withdrawals.

REGULATORY COMPARISON
Overview of stablecoin regulation in the US, UK and EU UKEU US
Regime Maturity
Key Timelines
Regulatory
Framework
US
The U.S. Genius Act
EU
Markets in Crypto-Asset Regulation
(MiCAR)
Backing Assets*
• 100% backing assets in USD cash or short-term
Treasuries (≤93-day T-bills).
• Non-significant EMTs –At least 30% reserves in
same-currency bank deposits, remainder in HQLA

• Significant EMTs - At least 60% of the reserves in
bank deposits; remainder in HQLA
• Systemic stablecoins – 30% unremunerated BoE
deposits vs 70% in UK sovereign debt (HQLA)
• Non-systemic – Full 1:1 backing in HQLR assets,
minimum 5% in on demand deposits.
UK
Multi-issuance
Stance
• Rules are still being fine-tuned; no specific focus
yet on multi-issuance.
• Multi-issuance under active review; EC considers multi-
issuance permissible, an equivalence-based framework for

EU/third-country arrangements is under legislative
consideration.
• BoE considers multi-issuance unsuitable for systemic
UK use. Preference for single-issuance models backed
by single reserve (incl. Non-GPB denom. stablecoins) .
UK Cryptoasset Regime (FCA)
UK Systemic Stablecoins Regime (BoE)
Eligible Issuers
• Bank subsidiaries, federal nonbank licensees, or
state issuers with federal registration, with
enhanced oversight for >$10bn circulation.

• EMTs: EU-authorised credit institutions or authorised
electronic money institutions. Restrictions apply to
non-EU currency EMTs (exceeding MiCAR
thresholds) as a means of exchange
• FCA-authorised firms for non-systemic stablecoins,
• Systemic issuers to be designated by HM Treasury
and jointly regulated under BoE/FCA oversight
Balance Sheet
Treatment
• Stablecoins treated as reserve-backed digital
payment assets, segregated in bankruptcy-remote

accounts and treated as cash equivalents
• EMTs are fiat-referenced cryptoassets subject to
MiCAR and e-money requirements
• (OCC/FDIC) Mandates prudential capital buffers
tailored to issuer risk profile.
• (Fed) Framework consistent with bank requirements
• EMI issuers: minimum own funds of 2% of EMT
supply; 3% for significant EMTs. Significant issuers
subject to enhanced risk-management requirements
• Systemic: 6 months operating expenses/largest

plausible loss event
• Non-systemic: 1% av. stablecoins in circulation
Capital Reserve
Requirements**

Marion Laboure is a Managing Director and Macro Strategist at Deutsche Bank in London, and a Lecturer in Economics and Finance at

She was previously an economist at the IMF, the Central Bank of Luxembourg, Barclays, and the European Commission. She has twice
received the First Prize of the American Society of Actuaries, was named a rising star in finance by Revue Banque, and is recognised

among the 45 standout women in fintech globally.
She holds a Bachelor's degree in Mathematics from the University of Paris Dauphine, a Master of Research in Economics from the
London School of Economics, and a PhD in Econometrics and Quantitative Economics from the École Normale Supérieure.

Camilla Siazon is an Associate on Deutsche Bank’s Macro and Thematic team in London.

advisory firms Eurasia Group and Greenmantle.
She holds a dual MA/MSc in International History from Columbia University and the London School of Economics, and a BA in History
from Barnard College, Columbia University.

4
• 財政期限的重要性上升，帶來政治紛擾：民主黨可利用具強制性的時間節點，作為政策讓步的槓桿。第一個要
注意的時間點是12/11政府支出授權到期，不過將期限延到2027年2至3月的機率較高(否則政府可能關門)。另
外推論1Q27將臨近債務上限，以目前的財政部存款和非常規措施的使用，最多可支應至2027年8月至10月。
• 財政政策整體趨於中性：分裂政府通常意味著較不具擴張性的財政政策，而眾議院掌握聯邦預算的撥款權，將
可能透過拒絕追加或直接限制戰爭預算的方式來迫使戰事降溫 。然民主黨同時也可能與共和黨協議，以撤銷去
年大而美法案的支出削減措施、恢復健康保險補貼等作為交換條件，讓白宮得以增加國防預算。綜合來看，短
線面臨預算協調的紛擾，美債殖利率波動仍可能偏高，但趨勢上政策制衡效果增強，有利減輕殖利率的壓力。
資料來源：左， 高盛，2026/11，右，高盛，2026/11。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11

關稅結構可能改變，資料中心監管程度提高但不影響建置趨勢
民主黨將嘗試撤回部分關稅，但整體稅率變化有限
政策影響
對資料中心監管程度將提高，但非禁止建置
5
• 民主黨將嘗試撤回部分關稅 ，但整體稅率變化可能有限 ：民主黨領袖已表示將嘗試撤銷已實施的部分關稅，但
川普政府仍可利用301條款推動新的關稅，整體有效關稅稅率變化可能有限，但其中可能的重點是降低對加拿大
的關稅。
• 對資料中心監管程度將提高，不影響建置趨勢：傳統共和黨票倉的德州變天，而德州是最大的資料中心建設地
區，新任的民主黨州長可能要求企業自行承擔電力及電網擴建成本 、保障居民用水優先權，並在保護規範明確
之前影響資料中心的建設進度。不過本行認為，除了要求企業揭露資料來源與訓練資訊、建立安全治理架構，
以及落實其他風險管理措施等規定之外，尚無太多州級監管制度被認為具有高度限制性。而在聯邦層級，大規
模的新監管措施即使被國會提出，也很可能被川普否決，且AI競爭力仍是跨黨派國策，建設趨勢將持續推進。
資料來源：左， 高盛 ，2026/10，右，高盛，2026/10。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10

©  CTBC
AI 資料中心已從「產業政策」轉變為「民生電價議題」
超大型業者算力供給轉吃緊
產業影響
2030年電力缺口收斂
6
• 德州資料中心暫停令，AI算力布局的轉變與供需影響：德州8月初頒布的暫停令，同步凍結了新資料中心的興建
（僅限未動工新案）與電力輸送。在扣除被淘汰的虛報專案需求後，預估部分專案將外溢至其他電力更吃緊的州，
導致全美電力缺口淨擴大約 3–6 GW。若Hinojosa 就任後延續暫停令執行超過一年，其影響2028 年全美電力缺
口將進一步擴大，2030年供需缺口預估將逐步收斂。另一方面，暫停令讓2028年本就吃緊的算力市場更緊，維
持算力租金的高檔區間，對超大型業者而言建置成本增加，與手上既有的算力可以提高售價，兩者相抵，2028
年企業獲利的預估影響僅在1-3%水準。
• 提高監管是正在發生的實質逆風，喊停是低機率的極端政治口號：AI 技術攸關美國的核心基本國力。民主黨的
政策向來主張「調整與監管」，而非全面「廢止」。因此，未來更嚴格的許可審查與補助限制，勢必會提高雲

端大廠在德州擴張的時間成本與資本風險。然而，也可能推動針對資料中心的特定稅種，例如聯邦總收入稅，
或針對資料中心電力消耗的消費稅。民主黨也有可能在新會期提出比共和黨更強制性更嚴格監管的版本 。民主
黨掌控國會後，最可能成法的是「電費成本分攤」立法，因為川普已將此議題列為自己的政績。

健護最具兩黨協商的價值，醫療產業相對受惠
12/11為醫療補貼可以放進談判的時間點
產業影響
健護未來一年的本益比仍合理
7
• ACA補貼及醫療補助是最可能與川普達成交易的領域 ：民主黨贏得參眾兩院，不等於民主黨執政，兩黨仍須協
商。就健護產業而言，相對有利影響的領域在2025年底已到期的<平價醫療法案(ACA)>增強型保費補貼，受淨保
費上升，已有超過200萬人放棄投保 ，及受<大而美法案 >被削減的醫療補助 (Medicaid)在2025~2034年超過
9,000億美元。現ACA進行增強型補貼的恢復提案還在進行中，需總統簽署才能立法恢復，可以是談判交換條件
的一部分；Medicaid刪減是已成法、分階段生效，無法被推翻，只能延緩Medicaid的刪減，其中對工作的要求
限制於今年底生效，早於新國會就任，12/11臨時撥款法案談判是可以納入談判作為局部修正空間的時間點。
• 健護是民主黨國會最直接的受惠產業，健護未來一年本益比仍相對合理：預期若醫療補助刪減延後，醫院將受

惠，ACA補貼若部分恢復，將有利ACA型管理式醫療業者的投保人數止跌回升。以健護未來一年本益比來看，
目前股價仍相對合理。

資金輪動而未明顯離場，美股短線有壓然無礙趨勢
期中選舉前股市較為承壓，但無礙趨勢
市場觀點
資料來源：(左) 高盛，2026/11；(右) 摩根大通，2026/11 。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11
分裂國會在兩年任期內的表現優於單一政黨執政
8
1974年以來標普500指數期中選舉前後的走勢
• 期中選舉前股市較為承壓，但無礙趨勢：根據歷史經驗，期中選舉年通常是總統任期中回撤較深的一年，且選
前的股市波動會明顯放大，然在選後不確定性消退，市場表現往往有所改善，標普500指數在選後的3個月，歷
史中位數報酬率為+6%，而分裂政府(總統和國會分屬不同黨派)的歷史報酬率通常較佳。
• AI相關類股波動高，資金輪動而未明顯離場，美股短線有壓然無礙趨勢：真正驅動市場的因素仍是利率、地緣、
AI，而不是單純從歷史規律推演，近期市場擔憂雲端業者的現金流和債務問題，因此若資料中心的建置時程受
到影響，將進一步影響相關業者的營收和償債時程，提高AI相關類股的波動；另外眾議院是否對川普提出彈劾 ，

也將造成政治上的波動。但就整體趨勢來說，既有政策不會因此出現重大轉折，穩健成長的企業獲利提供重要
支撐，AI類股修正時期資金仍在其他類股間輪動而未明顯離場，美股短線有壓然無礙趨勢。

©  CTBC
短線待消化北亞出口疑慮，長線行情回歸基本面
情緒回穩後聚焦基本面與地緣動盪 短線消化資料中心建設監管趨嚴對北亞出口影響，
然長線美核心政策延續性依舊，估計影響有限
9
• 選後反彈高度取決景氣與政策推演：回顧過去美股期中選舉期間，新興市場選前壓抑，選後大多繳出正報酬佳
績，上漲比率超越五成。以參眾兩院控制權皆翻轉的1994年、2006年來看，1944年因美聯準會啟動快速升息，
加上墨西哥金融危機爆發，選後新興市場進入近一年空頭走勢。2006年即便也是分裂政府，全球宏觀環境受惠
貿易繁榮、商品超級周期推動經濟成長，新興市場選後12個月累積報酬亮眼。總體來說，選後不確定性下降，
新興市場可望迎反彈走勢，而即便美國政治版圖翻轉，選後新興市場走勢取決於景氣、政策推演與地緣干擾。
• 市場短線待消化資料中心建設進程對北亞出口影響：美期中選舉對新興市場影響較大面向，主要是關稅與貿易
政策、債務上限，以及對資料中心建設的態度。關稅主導權在白宮推估影響有限 ; 預算與債限談判或對利率造
成波動 ; 短線需消化選後對資料中心監管趨嚴影響北亞出口的失望情緒，然預計長線AI建置仍屬國家重心政策。

• 期中選舉共和黨大敗，美元走弱：週三市場確認共和黨於期中選舉失利後，美元兌主要貨幣全面回檔。本次期
中選舉由民主黨取得參、眾兩院多數。民主黨掌控參議院後，可阻止或延後川普的人事任命；贏得眾議院後，
則可對政府展開調查，甚至進一步推動彈劾程序。歷史經驗顯示，當民主黨於期中選舉後同時掌控參、眾兩院
時，美元指數平均下跌 4.6%，6次中有4次下跌。整體而言，選舉結果短期內可能因預算協商及債務上限僵局

升高政治風險。2027年美國將再度面臨債務上限問題，美伊地緣政治及貿易戰等變數亦將牽動實質利率走勢。
• 2027年債務上限恐成兩黨鬥爭焦點 ，不利美元：12月11日前，現任國會通過持續撥款決議的可能性偏高 ，但
美國聯邦政府收支預計將於2027年觸及債務上限。受債務上限約束的債務規模預估於2027年3月接近法定上限
500億美元以內 ，之後隨 4月稅收入帳而暫時下降；但若財政赤字增幅高於預期 ，X-Day恐提前到來 。展望
2027年，美國財政議題仍將面臨逆風。共和黨失去完全執政雖有助抑制財政擴張，但政治僵局恐加劇債務上限、
違約及信評遭調降風險，使美元波動加大。

Document 510: セルビアは政権交代へ

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 27 日

MURC Focus

セルビアは政権交代へ

～揺り戻しの中で問われる対中関係のリバランス～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 総選挙で「学生リスト」が勝利 
西バルカンのセルビアで10 月 25 日、総選挙（国民議会の一院制、定数250 議席、比例代表制）が行われた。
即日開票の結果、先に辞任して総選挙に臨んだアレクサンダル・ヴチッチ前大統領は、自身が率いるセルビア進
歩党（SNS）を中心とする与党連合（統一セルビア）の敗北を認めた。代わって、ヴチッチ政権の汚職体質を批判
してきた学生を中心とする政治団体「学生リスト」が、国民の不満の受け皿となって勝利した（図表1）。

この選挙は、ヴチッチ前大統領に対する、実質的な信任投票の性格を持っていた。経済面では、 他国と同様
に、近年のセルビアのインフレは深刻だった 。一方、中国や欧州連合（ EU）からの相次いだ企業進出や人口動
態に伴う構造的な人手不足、政権による分配政策（最低賃金の引き上げなど）の影響もあって、経済は物価・賃
金スパイラルの様相を呈するに至り、国民の不満は高まっていた（図表 2）。 
図表 1. 開票結果 図表 2. セルビアのインフレと雇用

（注）開票率 96.7%時点。

〇西バルカンのセルビアで10月25日、総選挙が行われ、即日開票の結果、ヴチッチ体制への批判を展開して
きた学生を中心とする政治団体「学生リスト」が、国民の不満の受け皿となって勝利した。 
〇新政権に求められることは数多い。外交面では、中国との関係のリバランスが求められるが、 セルビアと中
国の経済関係が急速に深化したことも事実であるため、現実的なスタンスが求められる。 
〇経済面では、セルビアが決められない政治に向かうことで、マクロ経済運営の“受動的な健全化”が進むと考
えられる。一方、インフラの維持管理など、能動的な財政政策運営は望みがたい。

### Page 2

政治面では、政権関係者による相次ぐ汚職などが問題視されていた。とりわけヴチッチ政権と中国との近しい
関係が、国民の強い反発を招いた側面が大きい。セルビア憲法では、大統領を務めることができる期間は通算 2
期までとされている。そのため、すでに 2 期を務めたヴチッチ前大統領は辞任のうえ、統一セルビアの首相候補
として総選挙を戦ったが、有権者による信託は得られなかった。

今後セルビアでは、大統領（アナ・ブルナビッチ国民議会議長）が新首相候補を指名し、国民議会の承認を得
たうえで新政権が発足する。現状では、学生リスト所属の議員から新首相が誕生する公算だが、同勢力には明確
な単一リーダーが存在せず、政治経験のないメンバーが数多い。したがって、セルビアは“決められない政治”に
陥る可能性が高い。

2. 問われる対中関係のリバランス 
とはいえ新政権に求められることは数多い。外交面では、中国 との関係のリバランスが求められる と考えられる。
ヴチッチ政権と中国との近しい関係がセルビア国民の反発を生み、今回の早期総選挙になだれ込んだ以上、新
政権が表立ってヴチッチ政権と同様の親中路線を取ることは難しい。それに、中国と緊張関係にある米国にも、
新政権は配慮しなければならない。

また EU への配慮も欠かせない。ヴチッチ政権は必ずしも EU に敵対的ではなかったが、一方で EU と緊張
関係にある中国のみならず、EU と反目するロシアからも実質的な所得移転（安価なガス供給など）を受けていた
ため、友好的でもなかった。隣国ハンガリーのように親EU 路線への全面的な転換が果たせれば EU からの所得
移転は着実に増えるだろうが、国内には親中派や親ロ派も少なくないため、配慮が求められる。

### Page 3

それにこの間、セルビアと中国の経済関係が急速に深化し たことも事実である。対内 FDI（外国直接投資）の
ストックを確認すると、2020 年から 2025 年の間に、中国からの FDI ストック（図表 3）は約 20 億ユーロから 70 億
ユーロと 3.5 倍も急増しており、ストック全体に占める割合も 4%から 11%まで上昇している。同様に、中国との貿
易依存度も輸入面を中心に上昇が顕著である（図表 4）。

中国企業の多くはハンガリーと国境を接するセルビア北部のヴォイヴォディナ自治州に進出し、対 EU ビジネ
スに備えている。またそうした中国企業との取引を目的に、欧州企業や日本企業も続々と進出している 。こうして
形成された産業クラスターが、セルビア全体の経済成長を牽引しているため、新政権もまた中国との友好関係を
維持せざるを得ない。こうした点につき、現実的なスタンスが求められよう。

3. マクロ経済運営は“受動的な健全化”が進む 
経済面では、セルビアが決められない政治に向かうことで、マクロ経済運営の“受動的な健全化”が進むと考え
られる。そもそもセルビアの公的債務残高は、コロナショックを受けた2021 年をピークに圧縮が進んでおり、2026
年 6 月時点で名目 GDP（国内総生産）の 45%程度にとどまっている（図表 5）。この間に進んだ物価高に伴う“イ
ンフレ税”の存在も大きいが、セルビアの債務は削減が進んでおり、相応に健全な運営がなされている。

制度上、政府による財政拡張を中銀の金融緩和で支えるようなことはできない仕組みとなっていることも 、債務
の膨張を阻んでいる。 具体的には、セルビア国立銀行（中央銀行、 NBS）が物価目標（消費者物価の前年比上
昇率を 3%±1.5%にとどめる）に基づく金融政策運営を維持している。同時に NBS は、通貨ディナールの対ユ
ーロレートを1 ユーロ＝117～118ディナールで固定する厳格な通貨政策運営（管理フロート制）を堅持している。

### Page 4

このように、もとから比較的健全だったセルビアのマクロ経済運営であるが、新政権の下で決められない政治に
向かうことで、良くも悪くも経済のファンダメンタルズ（基礎的条件）の変化に即した 、ルールベースで機械的なマ
クロ経済運営がなされることになる。こうした意味で、セルビアのマクロ経済運営は受動的な健全化の道をたどると
予想される。

もっとも、そうした受動的な健全化は必ずしも良いことではない。グローバルな累積債務問題が懸念される中で
はあるものの 、インフラの維持管理など、経済活動を営むうえで不可欠な歳出も存在する。どの国も総量的な拡
張ではなくメリハリをつけた歳出が必要となるわけだが、決められない政治の中では、そうした意味での能動的な
財政政策運営は望みがたい。

また隣国ブルガリアのように、安定政権が成立するまでに解散総選挙を短期間のうちに繰り返すような事態も
問題である。いずれにせよ、セルビア政治が安定するまでにはしばらく時間を要しよう。

### Page 1

【結果概要】 
フィリピンの 2026 年 7-9 月期の実質 GDP 成長率（速報値）1は前年同期比 2.1％となり、前期
の同 2.4％から低下し、Bloomberg 調査の市場予想（3.1％）を下回った。民間消費が同 2.4％と低
い伸びにとどまり、総固定資本形成も同▲4.4％と減少するなど、民間需要の弱さが続いた。一方、
政府消費は同 6.8％、財・サービス輸出は同8.6％と高めの伸びを維持した。季節調整済みの前期比
では 0.1％増となり、前期の同 0.5％増からほぼ横ばいまで減速した。 
産業別では、GDP の約７割を占めるサービス業が前年同期比 3.7％と前期の同 4.5％から鈍化し
たほか、農林水産業は同▲4.8％と減少に転じた。鉱工業はほぼ横ばいとなり、内訳では建設業（同
▲4.0％）の減少が続いたほか、製造業（同 0.7％）も低い伸びにとどまった。一方、サービス業で
は行政・防衛（同9.9％）や卸売・小売業（同3.5％）、金融・保険業（同3.5％）などが成長を支え
た。

【支出別の詳細】 
民間消費は前年同期比 2.4％となり、前期の同 2.9％から鈍化した。内訳をみると、住宅・水道光
熱費（同 6.2％）や通信（同 4.7％）、教育（同 4.2％）などが増加し、衣料・履物（同 14.9％）は
高い伸びとなった一方、交通（同▲5.3％）が減少したほか、食料・飲料（同 1.6％）や保健（同 0.6％）、
宿泊・飲食（同 0.7％）が小幅な増加にとどまり、家計消費は全体として力強さを欠いた。 
政府消費は同 6.8％となり、前期の同 8.3％から鈍化したものの、引き続き高めの伸びを維持し

### Page 3

【今後の注目点】 
今後のフィリピン経済を展望する上では、公共投資の回復が重要となる。 2025 年に発覚した洪水対策
事業を巡る不正疑惑を受け、事業の審査強化や工事代金の支払い遅延が公共投資の低迷を招いてきた。
一般政府の建設投資は前年同期比 ▲15.1％と大幅な減少が続いたものの、前期の同▲ 33.4％から減少
幅が縮小しており、持ち直しの兆しもみられる。ただし、前年の支出減少による反動もあり、政府が進める事
業執行の加速が実際の建設活動の回復につながるかが注目される。 
一方、民間需要の回復には時間を要するとみられる。10 月の消費者物価上昇率は前年同月比 7.8％と
前月の同 7.2％から加速し、家計の購買力を圧迫している。また、フィリピン中央銀行（BSP）は 10 月に政
策金利を 5.50％へ引き上げており、高金利が設備投資や耐久財消費の重石となる状況が続きそうだ。ま
た、農業部門では生産コストの上昇や天候不順などを背景にコメなどの生産が減少しており、今後はエル
ニーニョ現象による干ばつが農業生産や物価に及ぼす影響にも注意が必要である。 
外需については、半導体など電子関連製品を中心とする輸出の拡大が引き続き景気を下支えすると期
待される。ただし、財・サービス輸出の伸びは前期から鈍化しており、輸入の増加によって純輸出の成長率
寄与度はマイナスに転じた。世界経済の減速や電子関連需要の変動が輸出に及ぼす影響には注意が必
要である。 
政府は 2026 年の成長率目標を 3.5～4.5％としているが、1-9 月期の成長率は前年同期比 2.5％にとど
まった。目標下限を達成するには 10-12月期に同
6.3％程度まで成長が加速する必要があり、達成
は難しいとみられる。公共投資の減少幅縮小は
景気を下支えするものの、物価高と高金利による
民間需要の低迷が続くなか、2026 年の成長率は
政府目標を下回る公算が大きい。2027 年に向け
ては、公共投資の正常化と物価の安定を通じて、
民間消費や投資の回復につなげられるかが重要
となる。

Document 602: 凱基投顧_3661 世芯-KY_劉宇程_20261110

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_3661 世芯-KY_劉宇程_20261110.pdf
Report source: line報告備份

Status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 10, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  IC 設計服務 ‧ 台灣

世芯-KY 
(3661.TW /3661 TT) 
2027 年營收倍增可期，2nm 接棒 2028 年成長 
重要訊息 
世芯-KY 3Q26 EPS 創歷史新高，並宣布再取得 10 億美元等級的大型設計案。
公司預期 4Q26營收再創新高，2027年營收將呈三位數成長，2nm專案亦將如
期於 4Q26開始 tape-out，我們認為世芯 2027-28年的成長能見度明顯提高。 
評論及分析  
3nm 專案 allocation 提高。美系 CSP 3nm AI 加速器已順利進入大量生
產，晶片符合所有測試標準且良率合理。公司預期此專案 2027 年營收將年
增三位數，優於先前指引，主要來自客戶提高對世芯的 allocation 。公司亦
表示 3nm 將量產約兩年才交棒下一代，加上雲端晶片走向對外銷售使單一
平台的生命週期拉長，我們預期出貨將延續至 2028 年並與 2nm 量產銜接。  
2nm 專案如期推進，地位較 3nm 更強。次世代 2nm AI 加速器已進入 final 
implementation 階段， 4Q26 起展開一系列 tape-out，目標 4Q27 小量量
產、2028 年初大量量產。2nm 採 chiplet 架構與 GAA 製程，設計附加價值
明顯高於 3nm，世芯負責包含 I/O die 在內所有 die 的 physical design 與整
合，且客戶目前承諾 2nm 晶圓將只由客戶本身與世芯兩家下單，世芯的量
產份額將有望優於 3nm。我們估計 2nm 專案 ASP 將達 3nm 專案的 2 倍以
上。此外，我們預期 A14 專案的 NRE 將自 2027 年開始貢獻，並估計美系
CSP 專案的 turnkey 營收將占 2027 年總營收的 68-70%。 
新取得 10 億美元等級大型設計案， 2Q27 起貢獻量產營收。 此案合約已簽
署，客戶為首次合作的美系企業，晶片用於 HPC。公司預期此案自 2Q27 起
貢獻量產營收並逐季延續，毛利率合理， 2027 年即成為第二大營收來源，
且屬可持續的新商業模式。公司表示產業工程人力全面短缺，許多專案需要
physical design 支援，世芯即以工程能力切入。我們在設計服務產業報告中
指出，客戶自建團隊的速度跟不上工作量成長，front -end 能力強但 back-
end 人力不足的客戶，最適合將 back- end physical design 與量產委由設計
服務業者，此案正反映此一分段委外趨勢。  
投資建議  
市場對世芯最大的疑慮在於營收集中於大客戶，以及世代交替造成的營收波
動，法說會兩項疑慮皆獲正面回應，而 關鍵在資產負債表可承接的規模。公
司指出產業持續走向 COT 模式，屬長期結構性趨勢且有利世芯，與我們在
產業報告中提出客戶由全包轉向分段委外的看法一致。我們調整 2026-27 年
EPS 預估為 124.20 元及 219.12 元，並預估 2028 年 EPS 為 409.40 元。我
們目標價 6,600 元係基於 30 倍(五年均值) 2027 年 EPS。目前股價約當 16 倍
2027 年 EPS 預估，我們認為評價具吸引力，維持「 增加持股」評等。 
投資風險  
美系 CSP 對 AI ASIC 需求轉弱；2nm 專案 tape-out 或量產時程遞延；新客
戶量產進度不如預期；供應鏈瓶頸延續；競爭態勢激烈。 
增加持股‧維持

其他專案如期推進，供應瓶頸逐步改善。 網通與 CPU 專案皆如期進行，我
們預期網通 3nm 專案於 2027 年量產，2nm 於 2027 年 tape-out 並自 2028
年起貢獻營收。中國車廠 ADAS 專案需求穩健，次世代設計將如期於 4Q26 
tape- out，我們預期採 5nm 車用製程。不過，載板與封裝產能優先供應 CSP
業務，使車用與消費性等業務出貨受限，公司估計若無此限制，3Q26 營收
可再多約 1 億美元，目前情況正逐步改善。  
毛利率稀釋並非負面訊號，營運槓桿推升獲利。 相較 1Q26，3Q26 毛利率
因 3nm 營收占比偏高、高毛利量產業務受限及部分 NRE 認列遞延而降至
20%以下，但受惠營運槓桿，營益率僅溫和下滑。公司認為 3Q26 毛利率已
接近此營收組合下的最差情境，4Q26 將與 3Q26 相近，長期營益率目標為
high- teens。如我們在產業報告中所述，評估設計服務業者應以營業利益金
額與 EPS 為主。我們預估 2027 年營收將年增 100.0%至新台幣 1,384 億
元，毛利率雖稀釋至 18.8%，但營業利益 、營益率將分別年增 90.8%及年
減 0.7 個百分點。

設計 (尤其是後段設計 )與生產服務解決方案。公司多年來深耕
IC 設計服務領域，團隊主要延攬來自於美國矽谷和日本的 IC
設計專家。 世芯- KY 總部位於台北，並於新竹設有 ASIC 製造
中心。基於全球佈局策略，公司在中國、日本和美國設有分
部。

### Page 1

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

法說會重點摘要

藥華藥 
(6446 TT, NT$ 1,335,  增加持股)

重點摘要  
1. 藥華藥 3Q26 營收 77.6 億元，QoQ +13% ，YoY 近翻倍，主因美
國與日本銷量成長、 Ropeg 注射筆於美國上市，以及美日原發性血
小板過多症（ ET）適應症自 9M26 開始貢獻；1 -10M26 營收 226
億元，YoY +86% 。3Q26 毛利率 96.8% ，YoY +5.2ppts ；營益率
40.3% ，排除約 4.7 億元員工認購庫藏股費用後約 46.4% ；EPS 
7.08 元創單季新高，1 -3Q26 EPS 18.43 元已達 2025 年全年的 1.5
倍 
2. ET 已取得台灣、日本、美國、中國藥證，美日皆為全線適應症，
韓國預計 2027 年初取證；真性紅血球增多症（ PV）方面， Ropeg
於 NCCN 指南高、低風險皆列首選，競品 rusfertide 僅列註腳。我
們認為 ET 可直接沿用 PV 既有的醫師網絡與用藥經驗，放量速度可
望快於 PV 上市初期，為 2027 年營收成長主軸；公司目標 2027 年
費用率降至 40 幾%，惟投資抵減用完後稅率將朝 20%靠近 
3. 原發性骨髓纖維化（PMF ）三期試驗 HOPE-PMF 已於 6M26 完成收
案 160 人、9M26 通過資料安全監測委員會（DSMB ）審查，目標
2028 年解盲送件，為第三個適應症；CML 、HCC、AML、CTCL 等
研究者發起試驗（ IIT ）持續延伸適應症。 2026-2027 年多項 PEG
細胞激素、雙特異性抗體、 ADC、 MPN 標靶新藥與 TCR-T 目標申
請 IND；竹北廠目標 2027 年投產 
結論 
我們認為藥華藥已進入 PV、ET 雙適應症驅動的成長階段，3Q26 在一
次性費用干擾下營益率仍達 40.3% ，營業槓桿持續發揮。我們看好 ET
藉 NCCN 首選地位與既有骨髓增生性腫瘤（MPN ）醫師網絡加速滲
透，帶動 2027 年營收增速持續高於費用；惟稅率回升將使淨利增幅低
於營益。後續驗證點為 4Q26 首個 ET 完整季度貢獻、 2027 年費用率
能否降至 40 幾%，以及 2028 年 HOPE- PMF 解盲結果

3Q26 財務表現  
 3Q26 營收 77.6 億元，QoQ +13% ，YoY 近翻倍，YoY 增幅由
2Q26 約+90% 擴大至接近 +100% ；成長來自美國與日本銷量成
長、注射筆於美國上市，以及美日 ET 適應症自 9M26 開始貢獻 
 3Q26 毛利率 96.8% ，YoY +5.2ppts ，原因有三：  
 美國、日本兩大高價值市場營收占比提高，產品組合優化 
 美國藥價於 5M26 調升，3Q26 為首個完整反映的季度 
 銷售以美元為主、成本以新台幣為主，新台幣較去年同期走貶

請將您珍貴的一票投給凱基！

### Page 2

台灣 藥華藥

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

 3Q26 營業費用 YoY +83% ，主因認列員工認購庫藏股費用約 4.7
億元（非經常性） ，排除後YoY +63% ；費用率由 3Q25 的 61.6%
降至 56.5% ，排除後約 50.4% 
 3Q26 營業利益 31.3 億元，YoY +168% ；營益率 40.3% ，YoY 
+10.4ppts ，排除庫藏股費用後約 46.4% ，與 2Q26 相當 
 3Q26 稅後淨利 29.2 億元，YoY +98% ，淨利率 37.7% ；淨利增幅
低於營益，主因 3Q25 認列所得稅利益墊高基期。 3Q26 有效稅率
約 10% 來自投資抵減，預計 2026 年大致用完， 2027 年稅率朝
20%靠近 
 3Q26 EPS 7.08 元，YoY +97% ，創單季新高；1 -3Q26 營收 YoY 
+84% ，營業利益 83.7 億元，YoY +149% ，EPS 18.43 元，已為
2025 年全年 12.3 元的 1.5 倍 
 1-10M26 營收 226 億元，YoY +86% ，2025 年全年營收為 157 億
元

PV：NCCN 首選地位穩固  
 美國 MPN 病患約 34.7 萬人，其中 PV 約 16.3 萬人、ET 約 17 萬
人、PMF 約 1.4 萬人；ET 人數隨診斷率提升持續增加 
 PV 已擴展至全球約 50 國，2026 年取得加拿大藥證，後續包括墨
西哥、越南、哥倫比亞  
 NCCN PV 指南中， Ropeg 為低風險 PV 唯一首選療法、高風險 PV
唯一首選干擾素  
 Takeda／ Protagonist 的 rusfertide 於 2026/8/28 獲准用於 PV，但
NCCN 僅列於註腳： 
 限頻繁放血或不耐受放血時使用，高風險病人只能 add-on 於現
行治療 
 機制為降低鐵的利用、只降低紅血球生成，不能取代細胞減量療
法；Ropeg 試驗評估的完全血液學反應（ CHR）涵蓋紅血球、白
血球與血小板  
 低風險 PV 中 Ropeg 合併放血對照僅放血：治療反應率 81.3% 對
50.8% ，血球比容（Hct ）控制 81.3% 對 58.7% ，疾病惡化 0%對
12.7% ；KOL 愈來愈重視疾病修飾與早期治療，有助擴大可治療族
群

ET：四國取證、全線適應症  
 過去 ET 用藥僅 Hydroxyurea （HU ）與 anagrelide ，臨床試驗中
Ropeg 對比 anagrelide 為 42.9% 對 6%，達統計顯著  
 ET 藥證：台灣 2026/5/12 、日本 8/24、美國 8/28、中國 10/15 已
取證；新加坡 10/12 送件；韓國預計 2027 年初取證 
 美日皆取得全線 ET 適應症（一線、二線病人皆可使用） ，後續各國
送件亦朝全線方向；日本 ET 病患約 6.3 萬人、美國約 17 萬人 
 Ropeg 注射筆已於美國上市並貢獻 10M26 營收，病人與醫師回饋

### Page 3

台灣 藥華藥

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

良好，有助提升病患留存與新病患採用

PMF 三期與適應症延伸  
 HOPE- PMF 為隨機、雙盲、安慰劑對照三期試驗， 2:1 分組，治療
80 週進行指標分析：  
 6M26 完成收案，超收至 160 人 
 9M26 通過 DSMB 審查，目標 2028 年解盲並送件  
 PMF IIT 兩年數據：血液學反應率 W12 為 61.4% 、 W48 為
63.5% ，其後達 70.0% ；平均血小板由 512 降至 279（×10⁹ /L ），
W12 起即低於正常值上限  
 IIT 延伸：CML（併用 dasatinib ）一期／二期 IND 已獲核准、HCC
（併用 anti-PD-1）一期數據已發表、AML 一期 IIT 進行中、CTCL
與 CMML 二期 IND 準備中、MF （併用 ruxolitinib ）一期 IND 已提
交；NCCN CTCL 指南已將 Ropeg 列為干擾素替代選項

早期產品線與產能  
 2026- 2027 年多項產品目標進入臨床一期：  
 雙特異性抗體 PD-1-IL-2v 預計 2026 年 IND 
 PEG-IL-2、PEG-Cytokines 、ADC 與 KRAS TCR -T 預計 2027 年
IND 
 MPN 新藥 Type 2 JAK2 inhibitor 與 mCALR antibody 預計 2027
年 IND 
 已併購加拿大 FORUS ，並於墨爾本設立營運據點推動臨床試驗；
2026 年全球員工目標接近 1,000 人 
 竹北廠已進入製程設備安裝與機電管線整合階段，目標 2027 年投
產；波多黎各廠設備均已下單，相關申請進行中

QA 
 CMS 追溯調整何時認列完畢？ 
 2025 年 CMS 追溯調整預期於 2026 年處理完畢，剩餘金額較前
兩次小；調整皆反映於營收，不影響營業成本  
 2027 年費用率如何展望？  
 1-3Q26 費用率約 51%，2027 年 PV、ET 雙適應症帶動營收成長
高於費用成長，目標費用率往 40 幾%邁進 
 不提供推銷、管理、研發細項預估，建議參考 1Q-2Q26 的費用
結構 
 稅率展望為何？  
 3Q26 有效稅率 10% 主要來自投資抵減，預計 2026 年大致用
完，2027 年稅率朝 20%靠近

### Page 4

台灣 藥華藥

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 4

 何時能達到規模經濟？  
 3Q26 營益率 40.3% ，國際大藥廠平均約 30%，前段班約 40-
55%，禮來約 50 幾%，公司仍有優化空間  
 資本配置規劃為何？  
 竹北、波多黎各廠依計畫擴建，並於美國購置不動產作為未來辦
公室 
 積極評估併購、少數股權投資與授權，並投資基金尋找合適標的  
 在保留發展資金的前提下，以股利回饋股東  
 ET 在美國的給付與藥價狀況？ 
 ET 適應症已納入美國保險給付體系，處方採用持續增加 
 ET 與 PV 為同一產品，批發標價統一，實際淨價依各保險支付方
的合約與回饋而異  
 ET 成長速度是否會快於 PV？ 
 9M26 即有實質營收貢獻； MPN 醫師手上同時有 PV 與 ET 病
人，且已熟悉 Ropeg 的安全性，加上醫學科學聯絡官（MSL ）團
隊支援，拓展速度將優於 PV 上市初期 
 PV 與 ET 互相帶動，並已耕耘社區醫院，加速處方滲透 
 是否考慮布局調控鐵 的藥品？ 
 公司認為調控鐵僅降低紅血球生成，產業趨勢是疾病修飾、降低
突變等位基因負荷，因此布局 Type 2 JAK2 與 mCALR 等標靶藥
物 
 Ropeg 在中國的定價策略？ 
 中國醫保走向較 commodity ，為避免影響國際定價，目前以自費
市場為主，並持續與主管機關溝通

⼤单⽇升幅。⻓⻜光纤、东⼭精密、深南电路等涨停。银⾏板块尾盘转跌，⽩酒、航空、

摘要：
A 股和港股的光模块概念股⼤涨，新易盛、天孚通信、中际旭创等⻰头股涨幅居前，中际旭创、
新易盛均创年内最⼤单⽇升幅。有报道称，微软据传正敦促特朗普政府放弃⼀项涉及特定光学
通信元器件进⼝限制政策。
11 ⽉ 11 ⽇， A 股午后震荡拉升，创业板涨超 3% 。 AI 硬件爆发，光模块、光芯⽚、光通信、光电
路交换机、电路板、覆铜板等概念股普遍拉升，剑桥科技涨停， “ 易中天 ” 均⼤涨超 13% 。煤炭、
海运等板块活跃，⽩酒、 AI 短剧、贵⾦属等陷⼊调整。银⾏板块尾盘转跌。
港股全天低开低⾛，恒指、恒科指⻬跌，恒科指跌超 1% ，权重科⽹股多数下跌，美团、腾讯、
快⼿、⽹易等下跌。光模块、芯⽚半导体等科技股反弹，剑桥科技 H 股⼤涨超 23% 。 AI ⼤模型股

双雄午后拉升，此前盘中⼀度跌 5% ，阿⾥官宣新⼀代千问⼤模型 Qwen4 即将发布。债市⽅⾯，
国债期货午后跌幅扩⼤。商品⽅⾯，国内商品期货多数上涨。

1.68 万亿，较上⼀个交易⽇放量 1715 亿。板块个股⽅⾯，中际旭创、新易盛均创年内最⼤单⽇

### Page 3

光芯⽚、光通信、 CPO ：微软游说松动禁令预期，⻰头放量全线爆发
今⽇最核⼼的主线，是光模块产业链的集体爆发。 Wind 数据显⽰， CPO 概念涨 8.25% ，光通信
模块涨 6.79% 。光芯⽚概念涨 7.68% 。
商品：国内商品期货多数上涨，截⾄收盘，航运期货涨幅居前，集运指数 ( 欧线 ) 涨 4.17% ；化⼯
品多数上涨， 20 号㬵涨 3.96% ；农副产品多数上涨，鸡蛋涨 2.94% ；油脂油料多数上涨，棕榈油
涨 2.57% ；⿊⾊系多数上涨，焦炭涨 1.56% ；能源品涨跌参半， LPG 涨 1.55% ；⾮⾦属建材全部
上涨，玻璃涨 1.48% ；新能源材料多数上涨，⼯业硅涨 1.18% ；贵⾦属跌幅居前，⻩⾦跌
1.40% ；基本⾦属多数下跌，铝跌 1.38% 。

### Page 4

⻰头个股全⾯开花。中际旭创涨 15.07% ，成交 312.29 亿元居全市场第⼀，总市值回升⾄ 6384 亿
元；新易盛涨 16.97% ，成交 252.34 亿元居第⼆；天孚通信涨 13.96% ，成交 102.04 亿元。
源杰科技以 20% 幅度涨停，剑桥科技、⻓⻜光纤、光迅科技均封住涨停，太⾠光涨 17.05% 、仕
佳光⼦涨 13.98% 、光库科技涨 11.91% 、联特科技涨 12.37% 、⻓光华芯涨 12.05% 。

### Page 5

这轮爆发的催化剂，是⼀则海外消息的传导。
华尔街⻅闻⽂章写道，驱动这波⾏情的，是来⾃⼤洋彼岸的⼀则消息 —— 微软正在游说特朗普
政府，要求叫停光模块的封禁计划。据观察者⽹援引媒体报道，微软及其他美国企业和⾏业组
织⼀直在向特朗普政府游说，警告禁令 “ 可能进⼀步拖慢数据中⼼建设 ” 。这类设备可让数据以光
速在数据中⼼内部传输，据市场情报公司 Cleanview 统计，微软在美国有 35 个数据中⼼在建、另
有 134 个处于规划。
中信证券在此前 8 ⽉的研报中已指出， FCC 拟禁中国光模块事件，以传闻版本落地的概率偏低，
美国市场对中国光模块的实质性依赖构成主要障碍 —— 中美光通信产业链相互依存度较⾼，北
美云⼚商的扩张节奏与中国供应商的⼤规模交付能⼒已紧密耦合，此类限制在冲击中国⼚商的
同时，也将推⾼北美云⼚商采购成本、延缓部署周期。
需求端的产业逻辑同样在强化。伯恩斯坦 11 ⽉ 10 ⽇研报指出，英伟达下⼀代 AI 计算平台 Rubin
Ultra 加速采⽤ CPO 技术，预计到 2028 年英伟达⽣态系统的光通信市场规模将达 468 亿美元，较

2027 年的 245 亿美元增⻓约 91% ；其中 Rubin Ultra 单个 8 机柜集群的光通信总带宽约 15.9 Pb/s ，
较 Vera Rubin 增⻓约 7.7 倍，单集群光学组件价值从约 100 万美元跃升⾄约 535 万美元。
基本⾯上，⻰头业绩持续兑现。中际旭创此前披露， 2026 年前三季度实现营业收⼊ 695.47 亿
元、同⽐增⻓ 178.13% ，归⺟净利润 222.38 亿元、同⽐增⻓ 211.81% ；三季度 800G 、 1.6T 等产
品订单较⼆季度实现较好环⽐增⻓，⾏业需求依然强劲，主要客⼾订单已基本覆盖 2027 年全
年，部分重点客⼾已给出 2028 年明确需求指引， NPO 、 XPO 等下⼀代光互连产品需求将于明年
下半年逐步释放。
新易盛则在互动平台表⽰已充分准备原材料及产能，预计未来⽑利率保持相对稳定。剑桥科技
亦透露，拟于 2027 年底前在现有产能基础上将⾼速光模块年产能再扩充⾄少 1000 万⽀。

### Page 6

“ 公司扩产计划正在按照节奏推进。 2027 年、 2028 年客⼾需求能⻅度和确定性较⾼。 ” 源杰科技在
2026 年第三季度业绩交流会上表⽰，产能分配⽅⾯，因 CW 需求增⻓更快，将更多向 CW 倾斜。
公司同时表⽰，对明年 NPO 及 CPO ⽅向的产品出货持积极态度，多款产品已与客⼾开展合作验
证开发。数通市场增量较⼤，以 CW 产品为主， 100G EML 产品开始出货并将逐步起量； 100G
PAM4 EML 产品已经批量出货， 200G PAM4 EML 产品客⼾验证⼯作稳步推进，更⾼速率产品也
在同步推进。
华泰证券研报梳理显⽰， 2026 年第三季度光模块及光器件板块总营收、归⺟净利润分别同⽐增
⻓ 96% 、 137% ，头部⼚商 AI 归⺟净利润持续兑现；展望四季度及 2027 年，全球 AI 算⼒侧投资加
码有望驱动光通信板块归⺟净利润延续⾼增⻓。银河证券亦指出，算⼒基础设施需求持续释
放，光模块与光器件环节景⽓度延续，头部⼚商⾼端产品放量、订单能⻅度部分已覆盖 2027

年， CPO 配套产品进⼊批量交付。
PCB 掀起涨停潮
与光模块同属算⼒硬件的 PCB 板块同步⾛强。印制电路板⾏业涨 5.76% ， 48 只成分股全线上涨
⽆⼀下跌。东⼭精密封涨停报 92.06 元，成交 126.54 亿元居两市第四，总市值约 1791 亿元；沪电
股份涨 10% 报 89.43 元，深南电路涨 10% ，景旺电⼦、奥⼠康亦封涨停。此外，⽣益科技涨
8.39% （成交 103.07 亿元）、南亚新材涨 12.46% 、⾦安国纪涨 5.31% 。
消息⾯上，不少 PCB 公司近期公开表⽰，受 AI 算⼒基础设施投资拉动， AI 领域 PCB 产品订单饱
满、部分产品交付安排较为紧张。市场研究机构 Yole Group 在最新《 2026 年 PCB ⾏业报告》中
预计，全球 PCB ⾏业营收到 2031 年将达 1400 亿美元，年复合增⻓率约 8.1% 。胜宏科技近⽇接受
调研时称， AI PCB ⼀直是公司盈利能⼒最强的业务板块，新产品进⼊上量阶段，随着良率提升

与产品结构升级，整体⽑利率仍有提升空间。

### Page 7

国⾦证券认为， AI 服务器、交换机及⾼速互联需求持续增⻓，推动⾼多层板、⾼阶 HDI 及低损耗
材料需求放量，单机柜 PCB ⽤量与价值量提升，头部⼚商⾼端产能保持较⾼利⽤率。
⽩酒调整
⽩酒板块表现疲弱，板块跌幅超 3% ，位居⾏业板块跌幅榜⾸。皇台酒业、今世缘跌超 5% ，古井
贡酒、⼭西汾酒跌超 4% ，泸州⽼窖、迎驾贡酒、⾦徽酒跌超 3% 。
华泰证券研报表⽰， 2026 年第三季度， 19 家⽩酒上市公司整体营业收⼊为 699 亿元，同⽐下降
10.% ；归⺟净利润为 243 亿元，同⽐下降 13% 。 2026 年三季度，⽩酒⾏业需求延续弱复苏，上
市酒企收⼊降幅较⼆季度明显收窄，预计⽩酒上市公司业绩⾃ 2025 年三季度以来的实质性出清
⾏⾄下半场。
银⾏尾盘转跌，常熟银⾏、平安银⾏、光⼤银⾏跌幅均超过 1% 。

### Page 8

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

3Q26 優於預期

維持買進評等，目標價下調至 219 元，潛在上漲空間 33%。 
1. 3Q26 獲利優於預期 
2. 2027 年將力求挑戰雙位數成長 
3. 今年資本支出 30 億元

3Q26 獲利優於預期 
啟碁 3Q26 營收 487 億元 ， 季增24%，年增 78%， 優於預期 ， 毛利率由前一季13%
下滑至 12.2%，主因原物料成本上升，費用管控得宜，費用率下降至 7%，以期末
股本計算 EPS 為 4.32 元， 優於預期 。前三季產品組合為營運商 （Service Provider）
佔 45%、企業（Enterprise）佔 31%、車用（Automotive）佔 24%。

2027 年將力求挑戰雙位數成長 
公司看好未來 2~3 年持續維持成長格局 ， 正朝著年營收突破60 億美元的目標努力，
2027 年將力求挑戰雙位數成長，針對 1.6T 或 OCS 交換器，預計 2027 年開始送
樣，目標在 2028 年開始產生實質營收貢獻，預估交換器業務在未來 2~3 年，每年
具備 70%~90%的高速成長機會。外在營運挑戰仍多，材料短缺與漲價從 Memory 
蔓延至 PCB、MLCC 及成熟製程 IC 等多種品項，預期供需失衡的波折期將延續至 
2027 年整年，另外美國政策變化 （包括FCC、USMCA 及關稅政策等） 將持續帶來
考驗。毛利率方面，由於材料漲價品項增多，合約定價若無法即時反映，或即使反
映也僅是轉嫁成本而非等比例加價，毛利率計算會受到擠壓。公司的目標是盡可能
維持在 12%以上，並力求毛利絕對金額持續成長。

今年資本支出 30 億元 
越南廠為營收爆發性成長的關鍵推手，下半年產能較上半年翻倍，生產規模已突破
百億，目前貢獻全公司營收五~六成。台灣廠區佔比接近三分之一。昆山 廠每月仍
有十幾億營收貢獻，墨西哥廠則發揮了重要的接單策略槓桿效果 。2026 年預計投入
30 億元資本支出，2027 年因越南與美國擴產規劃 ， 資本支出水準可能進一步拉高。

下修 FY27 獲利預估 
因毛利率下滑且市場風險升溫，個股評價下修，我們下修 2027 年 EPS 預估至 14.6
元，並下修目標價至 219 元，約為 2027 年 PER 15x。

富邦不同之處 
市場 Consensus 預估 2026、2027 EPS 為 13.23 元、16.21 元。

評價與風險 
啟碁為台灣大型網通業者，主要同業為中磊(5388 TT) 、智易(3596 TT) ，過往網通
族群本益比區間約為 10~20 倍 ，啟碁產品組合多元 ，持續拓展新市場，產業景氣回
升有助於評價提升。主要投資風險為：(1)戰爭造成全球景氣衰退、(2)電信標案容易
帶來較大幅度地營收起伏、(3)台幣大幅升值影響獲利。

富邦的股票評等標準 
評等  定義 
買進 預估未來6個月內的絕對報酬超過15%  
中立  預估未來6個月內有絕對報酬介於15%與負15%之間 
賣出 預估未來6個月內的絕對報酬高於負15% 
未評等 由於富邦目前與該公司有特定交易或沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等  
評估中 目前正在研議個股的投資評等，將於3到6個月內提供投資評等

產業評等 定義 
優於大盤 預估該產業在未來6個月內會比大盤指數表現突出 
持平 預估該產業在未來6個月內與大盤指數表現相較持平 
劣於大盤 預估該產業在未來6個月內會比大盤指數表現較差

This report has been produced in Chinese. In the event of questions regarding the accuracy of the translation, please refer t o the original Chinese version.

Fubon's Rating System 
Rating Definition 
Buy Expected absolute return to be over 15% within the next 6 months  
Neutral  Expected absolute return to be level within the next 6 months  
Sell  Expected absolute return to be over negative 15% within the next 6 months 
Not Rated (NR)  Pursuant to Fubon acting in deals involving the company or there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an investment rating  
Under Review Fubon is in the process of determining an investment rating and will assign a rating within the 3- to 6-month horizon

Sector Rating Definition 
Overweight Sector expected to outperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months 
Market Weight  Sector expected to perform in line with the broader market averages within the next 6-12 months 
Underweight  Sector expected to underperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months.

Source PDF: all report pdf/消費者物価指数（東京都区部・2026年10月） ～特殊要因剥落で表面上は鈍化も、物価上振れリスクは後退せず～.pdf

Economic Indicators

2026 年 10 月30 日

1/4 
消費者物価指数（東京都区部・2026 年 10 月） 
～特殊要因剥落で表面上は鈍化も、物価上振れリスクは後退せず～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

水道料要因剥落で小幅鈍化も、底堅い動き 
本日総務省から発表された26 年 10 月の東京都区部消費者物価指数（生鮮食品除く）は前年比
＋2.6％と、９月の＋2.7％から上昇率が0.1％Pt縮小した（市場予想：前年比＋2.6％）。季節調整
済み前月比では＋0.3％と上昇しており、表面上の前年比上昇率は鈍化したものの、物価上昇圧力
そのものが弱まった印象はない。 
日銀版コア（生鮮食品及びエネルギーを除く総合）も前年比＋2.6％と、９月の＋2.9％から伸
びを縮小したが、季節調整済み前月比では＋0.2％と巡航速度で推移している。また、米国型コア
（食料及びエネルギーを除く総合）は前年比＋2.4％と、9 月の＋2.7％から鈍化した。いずれも９
月からは上昇率を低下させているが、2％台半ばの高い伸びを維持している。 
10 月の上昇率を押し下げた主な要因は水道料の特殊要因剥落と米価格の下落である。東京都で
は水道基本料金無償化の実施時期が昨年の６～９月に対して今年は５～８月と 1 カ月前倒しされ
たため、９月には前年との実施時期のずれから水道料がＣＰＩコアの前年比を大きく押し上げて
いた。10 月は前年、今年とも通常料金となったことでこの影響が剥落し、水道料のＣＰＩコアへ
の寄与度は９月の＋0.25％Ptからゼロに戻った。これだけで前年比上昇率を前月対比▲0.25％Pt
(単位：％）

### Page 2

2/4 
押し下げている。加えて、生鮮食品を除く食料が前年比＋3.6％から＋3.0％へ鈍化したことも押
し下げ要因となっている。主因は米類で、前年比▲19.8％から▲31.3％へ下落幅が大きく拡大し
た（ＣＰＩコアへの前年比寄与度：９月▲0.15％Pt → 10 月▲0.26％Pt）。 
一方、電気・ガス代の上昇がこうした下押しをかなり相殺した。電気・都市ガス代は前年比▲
2.0％から＋2.6％へ上昇し、ＣＰＩコアへの前年比寄与度も▲0.08％Pt → ＋0.10％Ptとなって
いる。 
こうした点を踏まえると、10 月のＣＰＩコアが９月から0.1％Ptの鈍化にとどまったことは、
底堅い結果と評価できる。水道料の特殊要因が剥落したことに加え、米類の下落幅拡大も明確な
下押し要因となった一方、エネルギー価格が大きく上昇したほか、日用品や耐久財など幅広い財
で高い伸びが続いた。季節調整済み前月比でも＋0.3％と上昇しており、物価上昇圧力そのものは
弱まっていない。９月の急加速には特殊要因の影響も大きかったが、10 月の結果からも、財を中
心とした物価上昇圧力が引き続き強いことが確認できる。 
金融政策の観点では、今回の結果は追加利上げを急がせるほど強い内容ではない一方、物価上
振れリスクへの警戒を緩める内容でもない。財を中心に物価上昇圧力が続いているほか、先行き
はエネルギー価格の上昇も見込まれる。サービス価格が基調的に上向いているかどうかはまだ明
確ではないものの、日銀が近い時期の追加利上げを検討する環境は維持されていると評価でき
る。

財を中心に上昇圧力は強い 
電気・ガス代は前月比＋5.4％と上昇した。政府による補助は10 月分のＣＰＩにも反映されて
いるが、補助単価が９月分から縮小したことが前月比での押し上げ要因となった。加えて、これ
までの燃料価格上昇の影響が燃料費調整を通じて時間差を伴って料金に反映されたことも、電
気・ガス代を押し上げている。 
前年比でみても、電気・ガス代の上昇率は前月から高まった（前年比寄与度：９月▲0.08％Pt
→ 10 月＋0.10％Pt） 。政府支援は今年の方が前年より規模が大きいため、引き続き前年比を押し
下げているが、今年と前年の補助額の差は10 月に縮小したため、補助による下押し効果は９月よ
り弱まった。これに燃料費調整による押し上げも加わり、前年比上昇率を高める方向に働いてい
る。 
11 月分では夏の料金支援がいったん終了することに加え、燃料費調整の上昇も続くため、電
気・ガス代はさらに上昇し、ＣＰＩへの押し上げ寄与も大きくなる可能性が高い。一方、政府は
29 日に、冬場の電気・ガス料金支援を再開する方針を示した。電気料金については26 年12 月使
用分を１kWh当たり1 円、27 年１、２月使用分をそれぞれ4.5 円支援し、ガス料金についても支援
を行う（ＣＰＩへの反映は１～３月）。標準的な家庭では３カ月合計7,000 円程度の負担軽減を目
指すとしており、年明け以降の電気・ガス代は再び抑制される見込みである。 
生鮮食品を除く食料は前年比＋3.0％と、9 月の＋3.6％から上昇率が鈍化した。主因は米価格の
下落である。米類は前年比▲31.3％と、9 月の▲19.8％から下落幅を大きく拡大（前年比寄与度：

### Page 3

3/4 
９月▲0.15％Pt → 10 月▲0.26％Pt）し、生鮮食品を除く食料全体を押し下げた。 
一方、米以外では価格上昇圧力が残っている。肉類は前年比＋5.0％から＋6.4％、乳卵類は＋
1.1％から＋1.8％、調理食品は＋3.4％から＋3.8％、菓子類も＋3.3％から＋3.7％へ伸びを高め
た。外食も＋4.6％と高い伸びが続いている。飲料や油脂・調味料など一部では伸びが鈍化してい
るものの、米以外の食料品では価格上昇圧力が依然として強いとみてよいだろう。 
日用品についても高い伸びが続いた。家事用消耗品は前年比＋10.4％（９月：＋10.6％）と二
桁の上昇が続いたほか、家具・家事用品も＋5.5％から＋6.0％へ伸びを高めた。品目別でも、ト
イレットペーパーが＋17.2％となるなど高い伸びが続いており、原材料費や包装資材費、物流費
など既往のコスト増が小売価格へ転嫁される動きは継続している。 
耐久財の価格上昇も引き続き目立つ。家庭用耐久財は前年比＋5.7％（９月＋5.9％）と高い伸
びを維持したほか、教養娯楽用耐久財は＋6.3％から＋7.1％へ伸びを高めている。こうした動き
には、ＡＩ需要の拡大を背景とした半導体・メモリー価格の上昇に加え、円安による輸入コスト
上昇が川下価格へ波及している可能性がある。日用品に加えて耐久財でも高い伸びが続いてお
り、財価格の上昇圧力は依然として強い。 
サービス価格は前年比＋2.2％と、9 月の＋2.3％から小幅に鈍化した。一般サービスも＋2.7％
から＋2.6％へ低下している。一方、宿泊料は＋4.6％から＋6.7％へ伸びを高めたほか、 「他のサ
ービス」は＋2.8％で横ばい、通信・教養娯楽関連サービスは＋3.3％から＋3.4％へ小幅に上昇し
た。サービス価格は、一部に上昇の広がりを示す動きもみられるものの、基調として伸びが高ま
っているかどうかについてはまだ何とも言えない。今後の動きをしっかり確認する必要があるだ
ろう。 
こうしたなか、コアコアは前月から伸びが鈍化した。日銀版コアは前年比＋2.6％（９月＋
2.9％）、米国型コアは＋2.4％（９月＋2.7％）と、それぞれ伸びが縮小した。ただ、10 月は水道
料の特殊要因剥落や米価格の下落が全体を押し下げた一方、日用品や耐久財では高い伸びが続い
ている。サービス価格の基調的な加速はなお明確ではないものの、財を中心とした価格上昇圧力
は依然として強く、基調的な物価上昇圧力が弱まったとみる必要はないだろう。

＋３％超えが視野に 
先行き、ＣＰＩコアの上昇率は一段と高まる可能性が高いと予想している。これまでの資源価
格上昇によるコスト増は企業物価段階ですでに顕在化しており、今後も食料品や日用品、耐久財
など幅広い財で川下への価格転嫁が続く可能性が高い。足元では日用品に加えて食料品や耐久財
にも値上げの動きが広がっており、既往のコスト上昇の波及はなお続いているとみられる。 
食料品については、米価格の下落が引き続き大きな下押し要因になる一方、米以外では上昇圧
力が残る。包装資材費や物流費、人件費などの上昇を背景に、加工食品を中心とした値上げは今
後も続く可能性が高い。このため、米価格の下落によって食料品全体の伸びは抑えられるもの
の、先行きは上振れ方向を意識しておく必要があるだろう。 
エネルギー価格も今後のＣＰＩを押し上げる可能性が高い。夏の電気・ガス料金支援による押

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
し下げ効果は10 月まででいったん剥落するほか、これまでの原油価格上昇は、燃料費調整を通じ
て一定の時間差を伴って電気・ガス料金に反映される。このため、11 月以降は電気・ガス料金の
前年比上昇率が高まり、物価の押し上げ要因になるとみられる。 
一方、政府は冬場の電気・ガス料金支援を再開する方針を示した。電気料金は26 年 12 月使用
分を１kWh当たり１円、27 年１、２月使用分をそれぞれ4.5 円支援することに加え、ガス料金につ
いても支援を行い、標準的な家庭で３カ月合計7,000 円程度の負担軽減を目指すとしている（３
月にも実施するかどうかは今後検討）。負担軽減額は昨冬と同程度だが、実施時期が1 カ月前倒し
となっている。このため、前年比でみたＣＰＩには月ごとに押し上げ・押し下げの振れが生じる
可能性があり、注意が必要である。 
ガソリン補助については、政府が12 月から段階的に縮小する方針を示した。政府は現在、全国
平均の小売価格を170 円/ℓ程度に抑えているが、12 月以降は春先に向けて基準価格を毎月５円ず
つ引き上げる方針とされている。冬場の電気・ガス料金支援は物価を押し下げる一方、ガソリン
補助の縮小は押し上げる方向に働くため、今後のエネルギー価格は政策要因による振れが大きく
なる可能性がある。（ガソリン補助金の段階的縮小を巡る論点 ～物価上振れ・家計負担増と財政
負担のジレンマ～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所。） 
以上を踏まえると、先行きは既往のコスト上昇の川下への波及や企業の価格転嫁に加え、エネ
ルギー価格の上昇も物価を押し上げる可能性が高く、物価の上振れを引き続き意識しておく必要
がある。全国ベースのＣＰＩコアはこの先上昇率を高め、27 年初には前年比＋３％を上回る可能
性があると予想している。

Document 767: 米国：中間選挙アップデート（2026年11月） ～期日前投票でも民主党に勢い～

Source PDF: all report pdf/米国：中間選挙アップデート（2026年11月） ～期日前投票でも民主党に勢い～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

US Trends

2026年 11月 2日

1/3 
米国：中間選挙アップデート（2026 年 11 月） 
～期日前投票でも民主党に勢い～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  前田 和馬 
（℡：050-5473-3888）

（要旨） 
○ 2026 年 11 月の中間選挙において、連邦下院は民主党が優勢だ。予測市場では民主党が過半
数を奪取する確率が9 割を超える。 一般的に中間選挙は政権与党が苦戦する傾向にあるほか、
ガソリン等の物価高に対する不満は共和党への逆風となる。Reuters/Ipsos の 10 月世論調査
（22～26 日実施）ではトランプ政権の経済政策に対する支持率が25％に留まる。 
○ 下院選における選挙区割りの恣意的な変更 （ゲリマンダー）により、共和党は最大10 議席程
度を獲得できるとみられていた。一方、ラテン系有権者などが物価高を背景に共和党支持か
ら離れたため、当初想定の投票傾向が変わり結果への影響は限定的との見方もある。 
○ 上院は約 3 分の 1 が改選であり、改選議席に共和党地盤の州が多い。しかし、ミシガンやア
ラスカで民主党が優勢となるほか、テキサス・アイオワ・カンザスなども接戦となっている。
特にミシガンで左派候補が勝利する場合、2028 年大統領選における民主党の候補者選びにも
影響する可能性があろう。また、今後の関税政策の動向を見極めるうえでは、カナダとの貿
易が盛んなミシガンやメイン、農業州であるアイオワの選挙結果が注目される。 
○ NBC News の 1 日16 時時点の集計によると、郵便を含む期日前投票は5,483 万人分（24 年：
同 8,838 万人）に達する。このうち有権者登録の構成をみると、民主党支持が46%（41%）、
共和党支持者が35%（38%）、無党派などが19%（21%）と、民主党が優勢となっている。 
○ 劣勢を強いられる共和党は豊富な手元資金を活用し、広告投入などで形勢逆転を図っている。
また、一部のトランプ支持者は世論調査に非協力的とみられ、こうした人々の投票率が投票
日当日に上がる場合、直前の世論調査と投票結果が乖離する可能性がある。 
○ 仮に民主党が上下院で勝利しようとも、米国の経済政策が大きく変わるわけではない。上院
審議は過半数のみではフィリバスター（議事妨害）を回避できないほか、トランプ大統領は
議会を通過した法案への拒否権を持つ。もっとも、民主党が中間選挙で大勝する場合、トラ
ンプ大統領への交渉力が強まり、イラン戦争や社会保障費削減への抑止力となる可能性が高
まる（詳細は2026 年10 月 28 日付け「米国：民主党勝利で政策は変わるのか？」）。

Document 785: 美银之后，大摩也将英伟达移除“首选股”名单！

Source PDF: all report pdf/美银之后，大摩也将英伟达移除“首选股”名单！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

摩根⼠丹利与美国银⾏相继将英伟达移出各⾃的半导体⾸选股或精选名单，尽管均维持原
有买⼊评级，但⻩仁勋个⼈⻛波已令机构对公司领导层稳定性及⻛险收益⽐产⽣疑虑。下
周英伟达财报将成为市场评估⻛波影响的关键节点。
摘要：
英伟达正⾯临华尔街顶级机构的信⼼动摇。
继美国银⾏之后，摩根⼠丹利周三也将英伟达从其半导体板块 " ⾸选股 " 名单中移除，尽管两家机
构均维持 " 增持 " 或 " 买⼊ " 评级。这⼀连串降级动作，折射出华⻩仁勋个⼈丑闻对英伟达投资逻辑
的冲击正在蔓延。
摩根⼠丹利分析师在周三的研报中指出，⻩仁勋的领导⼒对英伟达的战略⽅向 " ⾄关重要 " ，⽽当
前围绕其个⼈的不确定性已令⻛险收益⽐趋于不利。此前，英伟达股价在周⼀和周⼆连续两⽇
分别下跌 4.3% 和 5.47% ，两⽇累计跌幅接近 10% ，本周市值蒸发约 4700 亿美元。周三美股盘
前，股价有所反弹，涨幅最⾼超过 5% 。
美国银⾏则早⼀步于 11 ⽉ 10 ⽇将英伟达、美光科技（ Micron Technology ）及泛林集团（ Lam

Research ）同时移出其 "US 1 精选名单 " ，但对三家公司均维持 " 买⼊ " 评级。美银分析师 Vivek
Arya 在研报中列出三种情景：⼀是影响可控、领导层保持稳定；⼆是不确定性持续压制估值；
三是领导层出现变动或监管限制收紧，届时将需要重新评估盈利预期。
据 CNBC 报道，⻩仁勋周⼀已返回公司办公室，试图传递 " ⼀切如常 " 的信号，并于周⼆继续在办
公室主持会议，为 11 ⽉ 17 ⽇的季度财报做准备。然⽽，市场对于其⻛波可能引发的连锁反应仍
保持⾼度警惕。
⼤摩态度急转，距重新纳⼊不⾜六周
摩根⼠丹利此次移除英伟达的决定，时间节点颇为微妙。
就在约六周前的 10 ⽉ 2 ⽇，该⾏分析师 Joseph Moore 刚刚将英伟达重新纳⼊半导体板块⾸选股
名单，彼时他在完成与公司管理层的路演后写道， " 我们认同并将该股重新列为半导体板块⾸
选 " 。
就在本周⻛波升级前的 11 ⽉ 9 ⽇，摩根⼠丹利半导体团队仍将英伟达保留在⾸选名单之上，并将
当时的板块抛售定性为 " 似乎过度 " 。然⽽短短两⽇之内，⻩仁勋丑闻的持续深化令该⾏迅速改变

⽴场。
⾃ 10 ⽉ 2 ⽇被纳⼊⾸选名单以来，英伟达股价已累计下跌逾 25% ，⽬前较 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇的历史收盘⾼
点低约 28% 。富国银⾏分析师 Aaron Rakers 虽于周⼆维持 " 增持 " 评级，但将⽬标价从 315 美元下
调⾄ 300 美元。

机构降级背后：评级与精选名单的区别
值得注意的是，美银和摩根⼠丹利均未下调英伟达的投资评级，仅将其移出各⾃的⾼信念精选
名单。这⼀区别具有实质意义。
以美银为例， " 买⼊ " 评级仅代表分析师预期股票将产⽣正向总回报，⽽ "US 1 精选名单 " 则是分析
师以声誉背书的⾼确信度投资组合。将股票移出该名单，意味着分析师认为近期⻛险收益⽐已
不够吸引，即便⻓期 AI 叙事逻辑依然成⽴。
对于被动投资者⽽⾔，英伟达的持续承压同样值得关注。在 VanEck 半导体 ETF （ SMH ）中，英
伟达权重约为 20.5% ⾄ 20.8% ，美光和泛林合计约占 8% ⾄ 9% ，三者合计占该基⾦约 28% ⾄ 29%
的权重。若机构资⾦持续轮出上述标的，被动基⾦的资⾦流出压⼒或将对散⼾投资组合产⽣显

著影响。
11 ⽉ 17 ⽇财报成关键节点
当前，市场⽬光已转向 11 ⽉ 17 ⽇英伟达季度财报。这将是⻛波爆发后公司⾸次公开披露业绩，
也是投资者评估事件对基本⾯实际影响的重要窗⼝。
美银分析师 Vivek Arya 所列出的三种情景中，最为悲观的⼀种是领导层出现变动，或监管层⾯收
紧限制，届时市场将不得不重新评估英伟达的盈利预期。⽬前，英伟达股价仍处于历史⾼点下
⽅约 28% 的位置，⽽华尔街两⼤顶级机构相继撤出精选名单的信号，令这场不确定性的边界更
加难以厘清。

英伟达新任董事会主席哈维 · 琼斯在邮件中表⽰，董事会认真对待⾃⾝作为这家伟⼤公司守

⼊。
摘要：
⻩仁勋离职后，英伟达新任董事会主席哈维 · 琼斯（ Harvey C. Jones ）于周四向员⼯发送电⼦邮
件，以下是信件的全⽂内容：
亲爱的英伟达员⼯：
我写这封信是为了分享⼀项重要⼈事变动。
⻩仁勋已辞去⾸席执⾏官及董事会成员职务，董事会已要求⾸席财务官科莱特 · 克雷斯（ Colette
Kress ）出任临时⾸席执⾏官，同时启动永久继任者的遴选⼯作，范围涵盖内部和外部候选⼈。
我将出任公司⾸位董事会主席。

科莱特担任⾸席财务官超过 13 年，是英伟达增⻓与资本战略的关键设计者之⼀。她对公司业务
及我们所处的市场有着深刻理解。董事会完全相信，科莱特能够兑现我们对股东、客⼾、合作
伙伴以及每⼀位员⼯的承诺。
⻩仁勋对英伟达的贡献意义深远。英伟达是全球最具影响⼒的科技公司之⼀。我们开创了加速
计算，定义了现代 GPU ，并打造了驱动⼈⼯智能的平台。
董事会认真对待⾃⾝作为这家伟⼤公司守护者的职责。
公司的实⼒源于我们的员⼯、领导团队、技术实⼒和合作伙伴⽣态。我相信，在科莱特的领导
下，在董事会的全⼒⽀持下，我们将继续推动业务增⻓，引领⾏业向前发展。感谢各位的专
注、专业精神以及对英伟达的投⼊。
哈维 · 琼斯  英伟达董事会主席
